# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 17 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 1
Om. Śrī 1. Homage to the great Lord of the Gaṇas.

Four Sanskrit verses stand at the head of the volume: वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ ‖*) यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyokhilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*) तृतीयाष्टकमध्यस्थः द्वितीयोध्याय आदरात् । व्याख्यातः सायकार्येण तृतीयो व्याकरिस्यते ॥ (*tṛtīyāṣṭakamadhyasthaḥ dvitīyodhyāya ādarāt | vyākhyātaḥ sāyakāryeṇa tṛtīyo vyākarisyate ‖*) वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायी सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyī sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

Ṛgveda Saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's commentary, Part 17.

Chapters 3, 4, 5 of the Third Aṣṭaka. (Sūktas 40–62 of the Third Maṇḍala and Sūktas 1–18 of the Fourth Maṇḍala.)

Third chapter of the Third Aṣṭaka.

All the sūktas of this chapter belong to the Third Maṇḍala. Since a detailed account of Viśvāmitra, the seer of the Third Maṇḍala, was written at the beginning of the previous, that is the sixteenth, part, it is unnecessary to explain it again here; nevertheless we shall say a word or two briefly.

This Viśvāmitra is one of the famous Seven Seers. In the Ṛgveda the Ṛks seen by this seer number 501 in all. About the seer Viśvāmitra, Yāska has told an ancient story in his Nirukta—

### Printed p. 2
विश्वामित्र ऋषिः सुदासः वैजवनस्य पुरोहितो बभूव । विश्वामित्रः सर्वमित्रः । सर्वं संस्कृतं । सुदाः कल्याणदानः । पैजवनः पिजवनस्य पुत्रः । पिजवनः पुनः स्पर्धनीयजवो वामिश्रीभावगतिर्वा । स वित्तं गृहीत्वा विपाट्टुतुद्रोभिः संभेदमाययौ । अनुययुरितरे । स विश्वामित्रो नदीस्तुष्टाव । गाधा भवत इति । अपि द्विवदपि बहुवत् तद्यद्विदुपरिष्कात्तद्व्याख्यास्यामः ॥ (*viśvāmitra ṛṣiḥ sudāsaḥ vaijavanasya purohito babhūva | viśvāmitraḥ sarvamitraḥ | sarvaṃ saṃskṛtaṃ | sudāḥ kalyāṇadānaḥ | paijavanaḥ pijavanasya putraḥ | pijavanaḥ punaḥ spardhanīyajavo vāmiśrībhāvagatirvā | sa vittaṃ gṛhītvā vipāṭṭutudrobhiḥ saṃbhedamāyayau | anuyayuritare | sa viśvāmitro nadīstuṣṭāva | gādhā bhavata iti | api dvivadapi bahuvat tadyadvidupariṣkāttadvyākhyāsyāmaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 7-24)

Viśvāmitra means that this seer is a friend to all, or that all are his friends; this is the literal sense of the word Viśvāmitra. This seer was the family priest of the king named Sudās, son of Pijavana. Once, taking the wealth given by that king, he set out to go to his hermitage and came to the confluence of the rivers Vipāṭ and Śutudri (the present Beas and Sutlej in the Punjab). Some thieves, wishing to seize the wealth he carried, were following him. Learning this, Viśvāmitra tried by some means to cross these rivers lying across his way, but they were very deep with the water of the flood and he could not. Then the seer praised these rivers, asking them to lessen their water and make it easy for him to pass; this is said in Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 3-33 and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-53-9 to 11. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33-5 this seer Viśvāmitra declares himself the son of Kuśika.

The Rāmāyaṇa, the Bhārata and other Purāṇas say that there was enmity and jealousy between Viśvāmitra and the seer Vasiṣṭha. Support for this is found in some places in the Ṛgveda too; it will be set out on those occasions. In the story of Śunaśśepha the part of the seer Viśvāmitra is very important. This story, told at length in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, we have explained fully in the third part of the Ṛ. Saṃ. Besides, descriptions concerning Viśvāmitra occur in Vedic works such as the Aitareya Āraṇyaka, Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, Taittirīya Saṃhitā, Kāṭhaka Saṃhitā, Maitrāyaṇīya Saṃhitā, Kauṣītaki Brāhmaṇa, Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa and Jaiminīya Upaniṣad Brāhmaṇa.

### Printed p. 3
## Sūkta 40 (Maṇḍala 3)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्र त्वेति नवर्चं द्वितीयं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं गायत्रमैंद्रं । तथा
> चानुक्रांतं । इंद्र त्वा गायत्रं हीति ॥ अग्निष्टोमे प्रातःसवने ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिशस्त्र इदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । इंद्र त्वा वृषभमुद्देवभीति तिस्रः । आ. ५-१० । इति ॥
> आद्या प्रातःसवने ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिनः प्रस्थितयाज्या । सूत्रितं च । इंद्र त्वा वृषभं
> मरुतो यस्य । आ. ५-५ । इति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The second sūkta, beginning इंद्र त्वा (*iṃdra tvā*), nine Ṛks, is Vaiśvāmitra (seer Viśvāmitra), its metre Gāyatrī, its deity Indra. The Anukramaṇī agrees: इंद्र त्वा गायत्रं हीति (*iṃdra tvā gāyatraṃ hīti*). In the Agniṣṭoma, at the morning pressing, the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin recites this sūkta as his śastra. The sūtra says: इंद्र त्वा वृषभमुद्देवभीति तिस्रः (*iṃdra tvā vṛṣabhamuddevabhīti tisraḥ*) (Ā. Sū. 5-10). The first Ṛk is the prasthita-yājyā of the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin at the morning pressing; the sūtra says: इंद्र त्वा वृषभं मरुतो यस्य (*iṃdra tvā vṛṣabhaṃ maruto yasya*) (Ā. Sū. 5-5).**NOTE**

Anuvāda: The sūkta इंद्र त्वा (*iṃdra tvā*) is the second sūkta in the fourth anuvāka of the third maṇḍala. It has nine Ṛks. Its seer is Viśvāmitra, its deity Indra, its metre Gāyatrī. The Anukramaṇī begins इंद्र त्वा गायत्रं हि (*iṃdra tvā gāyatraṃ hi*). This sūkta is applied in the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, in the morning-pressing rite of pressing Soma juice, to the recitation of the śastra-mantra by the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra इंद्र त्वा वृषभमुद्देदभीति तिस्रः (*iṃdra tvā vṛṣabhamuddedabhīti tisraḥ*) (Ā. 5-10); and the first Ṛk of this sūkta is applied to the prasthita-yājyā mantra to be recited by the priest Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin at the time of the morning pressing, as explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra इंद्र त्वा वृषभं मरुतो यस्य (*iṃdra tvā vṛṣabhaṃ maruto yasya*) (Ā. 5-5).

Om. Sūkta 40. Maṇḍala 3. Anuvāka 4. Sūkta 40. Aṣṭaka 3. Adhyāya 3. Vargas 1, 2. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9. Seer: Viśvāmitra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Gāyatrī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑ त्वा वृष॒भं व॒यं सु॒ते सोमे॑ हवामहे ।  
> स पा॑हि॒ मध्यो॒ अंध॑सः ॥ १ ॥

> *iṃdra tvā vṛṣabhaṃ vayaṃ sute some havāmahe |*  
> *sa pāhi madhyo aṃdhasaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 4

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । त्वा । वृषभं । वयं । सुते । सोमे । हनामहे ।  
> सः । पाहि । मध्वः । अंधसः ॥ १ ॥

> *iṃdra | tvā | vṛṣabhaṃ | vayaṃ | sute | some | hanāmahe |*  
> *saḥ | pāhi | madhvaḥ | aṃdhasaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंदौ स्रवति सोमरसे रमते क्रीडतीतींद्रः । यद्वा इदं सर्वं जगत्साक्षाद्दर्शयतीतींद्रः । तस्य संबुद्धिः । हे इंद्र वृषभं कामानां वर्षकं त्वा त्वां
> सुते ग्रावभिरभिषुते सोमे वयं हवामहे । सोमपानार्थमाह्वयामः । स त्वं
> मध्वो मदकरमंधसोऽन्नलक्षणं सोमं पाहि । पिब । इंद्रशब्दं यास्को बहुधा
> निर्विवेच । इंद्र इरां दृणातीति वेरां ददातीति वेरां दधातीति वेरां दारयतीति
> वेरां धारयतीति वेंदवे द्रवतीति वेंदौ रमत इति वेंद्दे भूतानीति वा । तद्यदेनं प्राणैः सम्यंधंस्तदिंद्रस्येंद्रत्वमिति । यत्र यत्र योऽर्थोऽनुगुणस्तत्र
> सोऽर्थः स्वीकार्यः । इदंदर्शनादित्योपमन्यवः । इदं ब्रह्म साक्षात्पश्यतीतींद्रः ।
> तथा चारण्यके श्रूयते । स एतमेव पुरुषं ब्रह्मततमपश्यदिदमदर्शमिती ३ ।
> तस्मादिंद्रो नामेदंड्रो ह वै नाम तमिदंद्रं संतमिंद्र इत्याचक्षते परोक्षेण परोक्षप्रिया इव हि देवाः । ऐ. आ. २-४-३ । इति ॥ इंद्र । इदि परमैश्वर्य इति
> धातुः । स्वमायया जगद्रूपत्वं परमैश्वर्यं । अनेनाभिप्रायेण श्रूयते ।
> इंद्रो मायाभिः पुरुरूप ईयत इति । इंदुशब्द उपपदे रमतेरन्येभ्योऽपि
> दृश्यत इति डः । पूर्वपदस्योकारलोपः । इदि परमैश्वर्य इत्यस्मादृजेंद्राग्रेत्यादिना रन्प्रत्ययांतत्वेन निपातितः । एवमेतानि निर्वचनानि द्रष्टव्यानि । आमंत्रिताद्युदात्तत्वं । हवामहे । हैयतेर्बहुलं छंदसीति संप्रसारणे लटि रूपं ।
> मध्वः । मधुशब्दः फलिपाटीत्यादिनोन्प्रत्ययांतः । आगमानुशासनस्यानित्यत्वान्नु मभावः । संज्ञापूर्वकस्य विधेरनित्यत्वाद्दुणाभावः । यणादेशः । अंधसः । अदेर्नुम्
> धश्चेत्यसुन् । नित्सर्वः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Indra" is so called because he delights and sports in the Soma juice flowing in the drop (indu); or else because he shows this whole world directly. The word is a vocative: O Indra, showerer of desires, we call you at the Soma pressed with the stones, to drink Soma; do you drink the intoxicating Soma, which is of the nature of food (andhas). Yāska explained the word Indra in many ways: he gives, splits, bears, or has the earth bear food (irā); or he runs to the drop; or he delights in the drop; or beings kindle him with their breaths, and that is Indra's Indra-hood. Whichever sense suits a place is to be accepted there. Upamanyu's school says इदंदर्शनादित्योपमन्यवः (*idaṃdarśanādityopamanyavaḥ*): he sees this Brahman directly. So it is heard in the Āraṇyaka: स एतमेव पुरुषं ब्रह्मततमपश्यदिदमदर्शमिती ३ । तस्मादिंद्रो नामेदंड्रो ह वै नाम तमिदंद्रं संतमिंद्र इत्याचक्षते परोक्षेण परोक्षप्रिया इव हि देवाः । (*sa etameva puruṣaṃ brahmatatamapaśyadidamadarśamitī 3 | tasmādiṃdro nāmedaṃḍro ha vai nāma tamidaṃdraṃ saṃtamiṃdra ityācakṣate parokṣeṇa parokṣapriyā iva hi devāḥ |*) (Ai. Ā. 2-4-3); that is, he saw this Person, the all-pervading Brahman, and said "I have seen this"; therefore the name is Indra, and the gods love the hidden. The root इदि (*idi*) has the sense of supreme lordship; supreme lordship is taking the form of the world by his own māyā. With this idea it is heard: इंद्रो मायाभिः पुरुरूप ईयते (*iṃdro māyābhiḥ pururūpa īyate*). Thus these etymologies are to be understood. The remainder is grammar.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—showerer of desires
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- सोमे सुते (*some sute*)—because the Soma juice has been pressed and made ready
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we pray and call
- सः (*saḥ*)—such (glorious) you
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—of sweet juice
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—the Soma juice, called food
- पाहिं (*pāhiṃ*)—drink

### Printed p. 5

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, since we have pressed the Soma juice and made it ready, we pray and call you, the showerer of desires. Do you, of great glory, drink this Soma juice, sweet and called food.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
We invoke you, Indra, showerer (of benefits), to the effused libation:
do you drink of the exhilarating beverage.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Indra—The commentator explains the etymology of the word Indra thus: इंदौ स्रवति सोमरसे रमते क्रीडतीतींद्रः (*iṃdau sravati somarase ramate krīḍatītīṃdraḥ*). When the Soma plants are crushed and the juice pressed out, Indra, seeing the drops of Soma juice oozing and dripping, dances with joy; hence the name Indra. Or: यद्वा इदं सर्वं जगत्साक्षाद्दर्शयतीतींद्रः (*yadvā idaṃ sarvaṃ jagatsākṣāddarśayatītīṃdraḥ*). Because he shows this whole world directly, the name Indra is fitting for him.

Besides, Yāska has given the etymology of the word Indra in many ways—
इंद्र इरां दृणातीति वेरां ददातीति वेरां दधातीति वेरां दारयतीति वेरां धारयतीति नेंदने द्रवतीति वेंदौ रमत इति वेंधे भूतानीति वा तद्यदेनं प्राणैः सम्यंधंस्तदिंद्रस्येंद्रत्वमिति विज्ञायत इदंकरणादित्याग्रायण इदंदर्शनादित्योपमन्यव इंदतेर्वैश्वर्यकर्मण इञ्छत्रोणां दारयिता वा द्रावयिता वादरयिता च यज्वनां । (*iṃdra irāṃ dṛṇātīti verāṃ dadātīti verāṃ dadhātīti verāṃ dārayatīti verāṃ dhārayatīti neṃdane dravatīti veṃdau ramata iti veṃdhe bhūtānīti vā tadyadenaṃ prāṇaiḥ samyaṃdhaṃstadiṃdrasyeṃdratvamiti vijñāyata idaṃkaraṇādityāgrāyaṇa idaṃdarśanādityopamanyava iṃdatervaiśvaryakarmaṇa iñchatroṇāṃ dārayitā vā drāvayitā vādarayitā ca yajvanāṃ |*) (Ni. 10-8). He himself has given the sense of this.

(1) दृ विदारण इति धातुः । इरामन्नमुद्धिश्य तन्निष्टादकजलसिध्यर्थं दृणाति मेघं विदीर्णं करोतीतींद्रः (*dṛ vidāraṇa iti dhātuḥ | irāmannamuddhiśya tanniṣṭādakajalasidhyarthaṃ dṛṇāti meghaṃ vidīrṇaṃ karotītīṃdraḥ*)—The root dṛ means to split, to cut. Irā means food. Food comes to us from herbs and trees, that is, from the plant world. For that plant world to grow, water is necessary. For that water, that is, for rain to fall, he cuts the cloud (with lightning, the thunderbolt); therefore the name Indra.

(2) डुदाञ् दान इति धातुः । इरामन्नं वृष्टनिष्पादनेन ददातीतींद्रः ॥ (*ḍudāñ dāna iti dhātuḥ | irāmannaṃ vṛṣṭaniṣpādanena dadātītīṃdraḥ ‖*)—The root dā means to give. He gives irā, food, by means of vṛṣṭi (rain); therefore the name Indra.

### Printed p. 6
(3) धाञ् पोषणार्थः । इरामन्नं तृप्ति कारणं सस्यं दधाति जलप्रदानेन पुष्पातीतींद्रः ॥ (*dhāñ poṣaṇārthaḥ | irāmannaṃ tṛpti kāraṇaṃ sasyaṃ dadhāti jalapradānena puṣpātītīṃdraḥ ‖*)—The root dhā means to nourish. He nourishes with the gift of water (rain) the plants and the like that give food; therefore the name Indra.

(4) इरामुत्पादयितुं कर्षकमुखेन भूमिं विदारयतीतींद्रः । (*irāmutpādayituṃ karṣakamukhena bhūmiṃ vidārayatītīṃdraḥ |*)—To produce food the earth must be ploughed. When the earth is ploughed, he makes it split with the plough and so makes it favourable to the crop; therefore the name Indra.

(5) पूर्वोक्त पोषणमुखेनेरां धारयति विनाशराहित्येन स्थापयतीतींद्रः । (*pūrvokta poṣaṇamukhenerāṃ dhārayati vināśarāhityena sthāpayatītīṃdraḥ |*)—As said before, he nourishes the plants and the like and protects them so that they do not perish; therefore the name Indra.

(6) इंदुः सोमो वल्लीरसः । तदर्थं यागभूमौ द्रवति धावतीतींद्रः (*iṃduḥ somo vallīrasaḥ | tadarthaṃ yāgabhūmau dravati dhāvatītīṃdraḥ*)—Indu means the Soma juice prepared from the Soma plant. He runs (hastens) to the place of sacrifice for the drinking of such Soma; therefore the name Indra.

(7) इंदौ यथोक्ते सोमे रमते क्रीडतीतींद्रः । (*iṃdau yathokte some ramate krīḍatītīṃdraḥ |*)—Satisfied with the Soma juice mentioned before, he moves about at ease; therefore the name Indra.

(8) इांइंधी दीप्ताविति धातुः । भूतानि प्राणिदेहानिंदे जीवचैतन्यरूपेणांतःप्रविश्य दीपयतीतींद्रः ॥ (*iāṃiṃdhī dīptāviti dhātuḥ | bhūtāni prāṇidehāniṃde jīvacaitanyarūpeṇāṃtaḥpraviśya dīpayatītīṃdraḥ ‖*)**NOTE** The root indh means to shine, to illumine. Entering the bodies of living beings in the form of the life-force, and shining while impelling them to do their respective acts; therefore the name Indra.

(9) यद्यस्मात्कारणादेनं परमात्मरूपमिंद्रं देवं प्राणैर्वाक्चक्षुरादींद्रियैः प्राणापानादिवायुभिश्च सहितं सम्मैंधन्नु पासका ध्यानेन सम्यक्रकाशितवंतस्तत्तस्मात्कारणादिंद्रनाम संपन्नं । (*yadyasmātkāraṇādenaṃ paramātmarūpamiṃdraṃ devaṃ prāṇairvākcakṣurādīṃdriyaiḥ prāṇāpānādivāyubhiśca sahitaṃ sammaiṃdhannu pāsakā dhyānena samyakrakāśitavaṃtastattasmātkāraṇādiṃdranāma saṃpannaṃ |*)**NOTE** Because worshippers, joining this god of the form of the Supreme Self with the sense organs such as speech and sight and with the vital airs such as prāṇa and apāna, conceive him in meditation as shining brightly; hence the name Indra.

### Printed p. 7
(10) आग्रायणनामको मुनिरिदंकरणादिंद्र इति निर्वचनं मन्यते । इंद्रो हि परमात्मरूपेणेदं जगत्करोति (*āgrāyaṇanāmako muniridaṃkaraṇādiṃdra iti nirvacanaṃ manyate | iṃdro hi paramātmarūpeṇedaṃ jagatkaroti*)—Idaṅkaraṇa means that Indra, in the form of the Supreme Self, makes (creates) this world; hence the name Indra came to this god, says the sage named Āgrāyaṇa.

(11) औपम्यनवनामको मुनिरिदंदर्शनादिंद्र इति निर्वचनवाह । इदमित्यापरोक्ष्यमुच्यते । (*aupamyanavanāmako muniridaṃdarśanādiṃdra iti nirvacanavāha | idamityāparokṣyamucyate |*)**NOTE**—The sage Aupamanyava says that because he sees this, that is, everything, the name Indra arose. Here "this" means the imperceptible, what does not appear visibly to the eye. For that very reason the Śruti says परोक्षप्रिया इव हि देवाः (*parokṣapriyā iva hi devāḥ*).

(12) इवि परमैश्वर्य इति धातुः । स्वमायॆया जगद्रूपत्वं परमैश्वर्यं । तद्योगादिंद्रः । (*ivi paramaiśvarya iti dhātuḥ | svamāyèyā jagadrūpatvaṃ paramaiśvaryaṃ | tadyogādiṃdraḥ |*)—The root idi has the sense of supreme lordship. This god has the supreme lordship of taking the form of the world by his own māyā; hence the name Indra. This idea is supported by the Vedic sentence इंद्रो मायाभिः पुरुरूप ईयते (*iṃdro māyābhiḥ pururūpa īyate*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-18).

(13) इनशब्दस्येश्वरवाचकस्याकारलोपे सति नकारांतमिन्निति पदं भवति । द्य भय इति धातुः । स च परमेश्वरः शत्रूणां दारयिता भीषयितेतींद्रः ॥ (*inaśabdasyeśvaravācakasyākāralope sati nakārāṃtaminniti padaṃ bhavati | dya bhaya iti dhātuḥ | sa ca parameśvaraḥ śatrūṇāṃ dārayitā bhīṣayitetīṃdraḥ ‖*)—The word ina denotes the Lord. If the vowel a over the n of this word ina is dropped, the word in remains. The root dṛ**NOTE** means fear. That god, the Supreme Lord, causes fear to enemies; hence the name Indra.

(14) द्रु गताविति धातुः । शत्रूणां द्रावयिता पलायनं प्रापयितेतींद्रः ॥ (*dru gatāviti dhātuḥ | śatrūṇāṃ drāvayitā palāyanaṃ prāpayitetīṃdraḥ ‖*)—The root dru has the sense of going, that is, go, run away. This god makes enemies run away; hence the name Indra.

(15) यज्ञनां यागानुष्ठायिनामादरयिता भयस्य परिहर्ता (*yajñanāṃ yāgānuṣṭhāyināmādarayitā bhayasya parihartā*)—He honours those who worship him with sacrifices and the like, and removes their fear; hence the name Indra for this god. In the same way many etymologies can be devised for the word Indra.

### Printed p. 8

Besides these meanings, the commentator says that in each context whichever meaning suits Indra should be accepted there. For example: इदं दर्शनादित्यौवमन्यवः । इदं ब्रह्म साक्षात्पश्यतीतींद्रः । (*idaṃ darśanādityauvamanyavaḥ | idaṃ brahma sākṣātpaśyatītīṃdraḥ |*) And in the Āraṇyaka it is heard: स एतमेव पुरुषं ब्रह्मततममपश्यदिदमदर्शमिती ३ । तस्मादिंद्रो नामेदंद्रो ह वै नाम तमिदंद्रं संतमिंद्र इत्याचक्षते परोक्षेण परोक्षप्रिया इव हि देवाः (*sa etameva puruṣaṃ brahmatatamamapaśyadidamadarśamitī 3 | tasmādiṃdro nāmedaṃdro ha vai nāma tamidaṃdraṃ saṃtamiṃdra ityācakṣate parokṣeṇa parokṣapriyā iva hi devāḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 2-4-3).

Here the word इदं (*idaṃ*) denotes Brahman. Since Indra sees that Brahman directly, that is, is able to see it, he is named Indra. After explaining the meaning in this way, the commentator quoted the Aitareya Āraṇyaka passage above, from स एतमेव (*sa etameva*) to इव हि देवाः (*iva hi devāḥ*), in support of it.

वृषभं (*vṛṣabhaṃ*)—This word is formed from the root वृषु वर्षणे (*vṛṣu varṣaṇe*). It means one who pours down the desired objects of those who serve him, as rain pours down. The word is generally used of Indra.

सोमे सुते (*some sute*)—ग्रावभिरभिषुते सोमे (*grāvabhirabhiṣute some*): in the Soma pressed with the stones. The meaning is: after the Soma plants have been crushed with stones, the juice squeezed out, and the juice strained and made ready, fit for drinking. I have explained the method of preparing the Soma juice at length many times before; see Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 10, pages 631, 637 and 697. For Indra's drinking of Soma, see Part 14, page 606.

मध्वः अंधसः (*madhvaḥ aṃdhasaḥ*)—मदकरमंधसोऽन्नलक्षणं सोमं (*madakaramaṃdhaso 'nnalakṣaṇaṃ somaṃ*). The word मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*) is an adjective of the word अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*). It may be given the meanings sweet, tasty, delightful, causing intoxication or joy. The word अंध (*aṃdha*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Besides, Yāska says of the word अंधस् (*aṃdhas*): अंध इत्यन्ननामाध्यानीयं भवति । तमोऽप्यंध उच्यते नास्मिन् ध्यानं भवति । न दर्शनमंधंतम इत्यभिभाषंते । अयमपीतरोऽन्न एतस्मादेव । पश्यदक्षण्वान्न विचेतदंध इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*aṃdha ityannanāmādhyānīyaṃ bhavati | tamo 'pyaṃdha ucyate nāsmin dhyānaṃ bhavati | na darśanamaṃdhaṃtama ityabhibhāṣaṃte | ayamapītaro 'nna etasmādeva | paśyadakṣaṇvānna vicetadaṃdha ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 5-1).

That is, the word अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is a name for food. Why? Because food is needed by everyone, it is prayed for (desired) by everyone; therefore अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) means food. Also in connection with Soma

### Printed p. 9

juice, the use of the word अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) in the sense of the Soma juice, which is of the nature of food, is especially frequent in the Ṛgveda. अंधः (*aṃdhaḥ*) is also a name for darkness (tamas). For in darkness nothing is seen, so nothing can be done. Even in worldly usage, people say अंधंतम (*aṃdhaṃtama*) when darkness has fallen so that nothing can be seen by the eye. He says that there is also an example of this in the Vedic text पश्यदक्षण्वान्न उचेतदंधः (*paśyadakṣaṇvānna ucetadaṃdhaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-16).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॑ क्रतु॒विदं॑ सु॒तं सोमं॑ हर्य पुरुष्टुत ।  
> पिबा वृ॑षस्व॒ तातृ॑सिं ॥ २ ॥

> *iṃdra kratuvidaṃ sutaṃ somaṃ harya puruṣṭuta |*  
> *pibā vṛṣasva tātṛsiṃ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । क्रत्युविदं । सुतं । सोमं । हर्य । पुरुस्तुत ।  
> पिब । आ । वृषस्व । ततृसिं ॥ २ ॥

> *iṃdra | kratyuvidaṃ | sutaṃ | somaṃ | harya | purustuta |*  
> *piba | ā | vṛṣasva | tatṛsiṃ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिनः प्रातःसवन इंद्र क्रतुविदमिति शस्त्रयाज्या । सूत्रितं च ।
> इंद्र क्रतुविदं सुतमिति याज्या । आ. ५-१० । इति ॥
> हे इंद्र क्रतुविदं क्रतोः प्रज्ञाया लंभकं सुतमभिषुतमिमं सोमं
> हर्य । पातुं कामयस्व । हे पुरुष्टुत पुरुभिर्बहुभिः स्तुतेंद्र तातृपिं तर्पकमास्यायनकारिणिममं सोमं पिब । पीतमिमं सोममा वृषस्व । जठरे सिंच ।
> यथा नाभेरधो न पतति तथा धारयेति भावः ॥ हर्य । हर्य गतिकांत्योरित्यस्य
> लोटि रूपं । निघातः । पुरुष्टुत । स्तुतस्तोमयोश्चंदसीति संहितायां षत्वं ।
> वृषस्व । वृष सेचन इत्यस्य लोटि रूपं । आण उत्तरत्वान्निघातः । तातृपिं । तृप
> प्रीणन इत्यस्माच्छंदसि सदादिभ्यो दर्शनादिति किन् । तस्य लिड्वद्भावाद्दिर्वचनादि ।
> संहितायामन्येषामपीत्यभ्यासस्य दीर्घः । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

For the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin priest at the morning pressing, this verse इंद्र क्रतुविदं (*iṃdra kratuvidaṃ*) is the śastra-yājyā; and it is prescribed in the sūtra इंद्र क्रतुविदं सुतमिति याज्या (*iṃdra kratuvidaṃ sutamiti yājyā*) (Ā. 5-10). "O Indra, desire to drink this pressed Soma, the obtainer of kratu, that is, of wisdom (prajñā). O Indra, praised by many, drink this Soma, which satisfies and refreshes. Having drunk it, pour it into the belly; the sense is: hold it so that it does not fall below the navel."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुष्टुत (*puruṣṭuta*)—praised by many
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- क्रतुविदं (*kratuvidaṃ*)—the giver of wisdom
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—pressed
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—the Soma juice
- हर्य (*harya*)—desire to drink

### Printed p. 10

- तातृपिं (*tātṛpiṃ*)—this Soma, which gives satisfaction
- पिब (*piba*)—drink
- आ वृषस्व (*ā vṛṣasva*)—pour it into the belly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, praised by many, desire to drink the Soma juice, which gives wisdom and is ready, pressed. Drink this satisfying juice and fill your belly.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, the praised of many, accept the effused Soma juice, the conferrer of knowledge ; drink ; imbibe the satisfactory draught.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

At the morning pressing the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin is to use this verse इंद्र क्रतुविदं (*iṃdra kratuvidaṃ*) as a śastra-yājyā mantra; this is stated by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra इंद्र क्रतुविदं सुतमिति याज्या (*iṃdra kratuvidaṃ sutamiti yājyā*).

क्रतुविदं (*kratuvidaṃ*)—For the word क्रतु (*kratu*) Yāska gave the explanation क्रतुं कर्म वा प्रज्ञां ना (*kratuṃ karma vā prajñāṃ nā*) (Ni. 2-28). Moreover the word क्रतु (*kratu*) is read among the twenty-six names of action beginning with अपः (*apaḥ*) and अष्टः (*aṣṭaḥ*) (Ni. 3-1), and among the eleven names of wisdom beginning with केतः (*ketaḥ*) and केतुः (*ketuḥ*) (Ni. 3-13). Therefore क्रतुविदं (*kratuvidaṃ*) means प्रज्ञया लंभकं (*prajñayā laṃbhakaṃ*): that which can be obtained by wisdom, by knowledge. The meaning is that proper understanding or knowledge is needed to prepare the Soma juice. Or, if the word क्रतु (*kratu*) is taken to mean action, it may mean the Soma juice prepared in the sacrificial action. The sense is: such a Soma juice.

सुतं सोमं (*sutaṃ somaṃ*)—The use of the word सुतं (*sutaṃ*) here means the Soma juice that has just now, in this sacrifice, been freshly prepared: the Soma plants crushed, the juice squeezed out, strained and made ready, fit for drinking.

पुरुष्टुत (*puruṣṭuta*)—पुरुभिर्बहुभिः स्तुतेंद्र (*purubhirbahubhiḥ stuteṃdra*): Indra, who is praised by many people. Many people, priests, sacrificers and others, praise Indra.

पिब (*piba*)—The final syllable of this word is made long in the Saṃhitā text for the convenience of the metre. Such usage is frequent in the Ṛgveda. For example, the word पिब (*piba*) in पिबा सोममभि (*pibā somamabhi*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-17-1); पिबा सोममिंद्र मंदतु (*pibā somamiṃdra maṃdatu*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-22-1); पिबा सोमं मदाय (*pibā somaṃ madāya*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-95-3); पिबा सोमं महते (*pibā somaṃ mahate*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-116-1); the verse **NOTE**

### Printed p. 11

The word श्रुधि (*śrudhi*) too is used with a long final syllable in verses such as श्रुधी नो अग्ने सदने (*śrudhī no agne sadane*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-11-9), श्रुधी न इंद्र (*śrudhī na iṃdra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-26-1), श्रुधी हवमिंद्र शूर (*śrudhī havamiṃdra śūra*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-148-5), श्रुधी हवं तिरश्चाशि (*śrudhī havaṃ tiraścāśi*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-95-4), and so on. There are many such examples.

तातृपिं (*tātṛpiṃ*)—तर्पकमाप्यायनकारिणमिमं सोमं (*tarpakamāpyāyanakāriṇamimaṃ somaṃ*): the Soma that causes satisfaction; that is, the Soma juice which, after being drunk, settles in the belly, cools it and makes joy arise. There are many descriptions that drinking Soma juice produces satisfaction or joy and also intoxication.

वृषस्व (*vṛṣasva*)—जठरे सिंच । यथा नाभेरधो न पतति तथा धारयेति भावः । (*jaṭhare siṃca | yathā nābheradho na patati tathā dhārayeti bhāvaḥ |*) In this verse it is plainly said पिब (*piba*), "drink the Soma juice"; is not the use of the word वृषस्व (*vṛṣasva*) then useless? The answer: since Indra is famous as extremely fond of drinking Soma juice, the use of the two words पिब (*piba*) and वृषभ (*vṛṣabha*) **NOTE** is also proper, to stress plainly: "when drinking Soma, drink as much as you wish, so that it does not fall below the navel, and drink so that the inner part of the belly is well soaked and refreshed."

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ प्र णो॑ धि॒तावा॑नं य॒ज्ञं विश्वे॑भिर्दे॒वेभिः॑ ।  
> ति॒रः स्त॑वान विश्वते ॥ ३ ॥

> *iṃdra pra ṇo dhitāvānaṃ yajñaṃ viśvebhirdevebhiḥ |*  
> *tiraḥ stavāna viśvate ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । प्र । नः । धितवानं । यज्ञं । विश्वेभिः । देवेभिः ।  
> तिरः । स्तवान । विश्वते ॥ ३ ॥

> *iṃdra | pra | naḥ | dhitavānaṃ | yajñaṃ | viśvebhiḥ | devebhiḥ |*  
> *tiraḥ | stavāna | viśvate ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तवान स्तोतृभिः स्तूयमान विश्वते विशां मरुतां पते हे इंद्र विश्वेभिर्देवेभिः सर्वैर्यजनीयैर्देवैः सहितस्तं धितावानं । वन्यते संभज्यत इति
> वानं हविः । धितं निहितं हविर्यस्य तं । संभृतहविष्कमित्यर्थः । अस्मदीयमिमं
> यज्ञं प्र तिरः । प्रकर्षेण वर्धय । हविःस्वीकरणेन संपूर्णं कुर्विति भावः ॥ णः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, praised by singers (स्तवान (*stavāna*)), lord of the विश् (*viś*), that is, of the Maruts (विश्वते (*viśvate*)): together with all the gods worthy of sacrifice, प्र तिरः (*pra tiraḥ*), make grow vigorously this sacrifice of ours, which is धितावानं (*dhitāvānaṃ*)." वानं (*vānaṃ*) is the oblation, because it is shared out (वन्यते संभज्यते (*vanyate saṃbhajyate*)); धितं (*dhitaṃ*) means placed; so the sacrifice is one in which the oblation has been placed, that is, in which the oblation has been gathered. "Make it grow" means: complete it by accepting the oblation.

### Printed p. 12

> उपसर्गाद्बहुलमिति संहितायां णत्वं । धितावानं । डुधाण् धारणपोषणयोरित्यस्मान्निष्ठा । बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदस्वरः । तिरः । तरतेर्व्यत्ययेन शः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> स्तवान । ष्टुञ् स्तुत् । सम्यानच् स्तुव इत्युपपदाभावेऽपि कर्मण्यानच् प्रत्ययः ।
> आमंत्रितत्वान्निघातः ॥

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- स्तवान (*stavāna*)—he who is praised (by the devotees)
- विश्वते (*viśvate*)—lord of the Maruts, who are of the nature of subjects
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- विश्वेभिः देवेभिः (*viśvebhiḥ devebhiḥ*)—together with all the gods
- धितावानं (*dhitāvānaṃ*)—furnished with oblation
- नः यज्ञं (*naḥ yajñaṃ*)—this sacrifice of ours
- प्रतिर (*pratira*)—make it grow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who are praised by the devotees and are lord of the Maruts, who are of the nature of subjects, together with all the gods make this sacrifice of ours, furnished with oblation, grow.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, lord of men, you are praised (by the devout) and aided by the gods ; perfect this our sacrifice in which the oblation is offered (to you).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

विश्वते (*viśvate*)—विशां मरुतां पते (*viśāṃ marutāṃ pate*): Indra, the leader of the Marut gods. There is a very important companionship between the Marut gods and Indra. We have explained this at length in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 8, page 172, and Part 13, page 210.

The eternal companionship of Indra and the Maruts is well known from the Śruti. The relation of king and subjects between Indra and the Maruts, the offering of oblations to Indra together with the Maruts in the principal sacrificial acts, and the Maruts' helping Indra when he fights with the demon Vṛtra, are described in many places in the Saṃhitās and the Brāhmaṇas.

क्षत्रं वा इंद्रो विशो मरुतो विशा वै क्षत्रियो बलवान् भवति ॥ (*kṣatraṃ vā iṃdro viśo maruto viśā vai kṣatriyo balavān bhavati ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 4-3-3-6).

Indra is the king of kṣatriya splendour, the Maruts are the subjects; it is indicated here that the king becomes powerful only through the support of the subjects, and that through the help of the Maruts Indra's valour increased.

### Printed p. 13

मरुद्दिः सह यत्रेंद्रो मरुत्वांस्तत्र सोऽभवत् । (*maruddiḥ saha yatreṃdro marutvāṃstatra so 'bhavat |*) (Bṛ. De. 4-56).

The Bṛhaddevatā explains this adjective, which indicates the relation of these deities: wherever Indra together with the Maruts is addressed, he is called मरुत्वान् (*marutvān*).

धितानानं (*dhitānānaṃ*)—वन्यते संभज्यते इति वानं हविः । धितं निहितं हविर्यस्य तं । संभृतहविष्यमित्यर्थः । (*vanyate saṃbhajyate iti vānaṃ haviḥ | dhitaṃ nihitaṃ haviryasya taṃ | saṃbhṛtahaviṣyamityarthaḥ |*) The word वानं (*vānaṃ*), formed from the root वन षण संभक्तौ (*vana ṣaṇa saṃbhaktau*), means a thing prepared. The sense is: the action, that is, the sacrifice, in which such a thing, that is, an oblation, has been set down to be offered to the gods. In the sacrifice the oblation is prepared and kept ready to offer to the gods. The meaning of the word is: a sacrifice furnished with such an oblation.

प्रति (*prati*)—Accept the oblation we offer and make this sacrifice grow, make it complete.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्र॒ सोमाः॑ सु॒ता इ॒मे तव॒ प्र यं॑ति सत्पते ।  
> क्षयं॑ चं॒द्रास॒ इंद॑वः ॥ ४ ॥

> *iṃdra somāḥ sutā ime tava pra yaṃti satpate |*  
> *kṣayaṃ caṃdrāsa iṃdavaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्र । सोमाः । सुताः । इमे । तव । प्र । यंति । सत्पते ।  
> क्षयं । चंद्रासः । इंदवः ॥ ४ ॥

> *iṃdra | somāḥ | sutāḥ | ime | tava | pra | yaṃti | satpate |*  
> *kṣayaṃ | caṃdrāsaḥ | iṃdavaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सत्पते इंद्र चंद्रास आह्लादयितार इंदवो दीप्ताः सुता अभिषुता
> इमेऽस्माभिर्दत्ताः सोमाः क्षयं । क्षियंति निवसंत्यन्नादीन्यत्रेति क्षयो जठरं ।
> प्र यंति । प्रकर्षण गच्छंति । तान् धारयेति भावः ॥ क्षयं । क्षि निवासगत्योरित्यस्मादधिकरणे घप्रत्ययः । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतोदात्ते प्राप्ते क्षयो निवास इत्याद्युदात्तत्वं । चंद्रासः । चदि आह्लादने दीप्ता च । अस्मात्सा यितंचीत्यादिना रक्षत्ययः । आज्ञसेरसुगित्यसुक् । प्रत्ययस्वरः । इंदवः । उंदेरिच्चा देरित्युप्रत्ययः ।
> निदित्यनुवृत्तेराद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, lord of the good (सत्पते (*satpate*)): these Soma juices, gladdening (चंद्रासः (*caṃdrāsaḥ*)) and bright (इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)), pressed and offered by us, go forth (प्र यंति (*pra yaṃti*)) into क्षयं (*kṣayaṃ*)." क्षय (*kṣaya*) is the belly, since food and the like dwell (क्षियंति (*kṣiyaṃti*)) in it; प्र यंति (*pra yaṃti*) means they go forth vigorously. The sense is: hold them. चंद्रासः (*caṃdrāsaḥ*) is from चदि (*cadi*), in the sense of gladdening and shining.

### Printed p. 14

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सत्पते (*satpate*)—O lord of the virtuous
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—Indra
- चंद्रासः (*caṃdrāsaḥ*)—delighting
- इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)—brilliant
- सुताः (*sutāḥ*)—pressed
- इमे (*ime*)—offered by us
- सोमाः (*somāḥ*)—these Soma juices
- तव (*tava*)—your
- क्षयं (*kṣayaṃ*)—towards the dwelling-place in the form of the stomach
- प्र यंति (*pra yaṃti*)—flow forth (in streams)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, protector of the virtuous, these Soma juices, delighting, brilliant, pressed and offered by us, flow towards your dwelling-place, your stomach.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, lord of the virtuous, these effused Soma juices, exhilarating and brilliant, proceed to your abode (stomach)

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

चंद्रासः (*caṃdrāsaḥ*)—The word चंद्र (*caṃdra*), formed from the root चदि आह्लादने (*cadi āhlādane*), means delightful, giving joy. This word is an adjective of the word सोमाः (*somāḥ*).

इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)—The word इंदु (*iṃdu*), formed from the root उंदी क्लेदने (*uṃdī kledane*), means oozing, dripping. When the Soma plants are crushed with a stone and the juice is taken out, the Soma juice drips little by little, just as juice oozes when sugarcane is pressed; so it is the custom to call freshly pressed Soma juice इंदु (*iṃdu*) or इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*) (plural). Since it has just been pressed, the sense "shining" can also be given.

क्षयं (*kṣayaṃ*)—The dwelling-place. Here the dwelling-place means Indra's belly. After Indra drinks the Soma juice it goes into his belly and settles there; hence that stomach is designated here as क्षयं (*kṣayaṃ*), the dwelling-place.

इमे सुताः (*ime sutāḥ*)—These Soma juices that we are pressing now in the sacrifice; that is, not juices pressed earlier. The sense is: the Soma juice freshly pressed now.

सोमाः सुताः (*somāḥ sutāḥ*)—These are words ending in the nominative plural. The earlier forms of these words, सोमासः सुतासः (*somāsaḥ sutāsaḥ*), are also much used in the Ṛgveda.

### Printed p. 15
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> द॒धि॒ष्वा ज॒ठरे॑ सु॒तं सोम॑मिंद्र॒ वरे॑ण्यं ।  
> तव॑ द्यु॒क्ष्यास॒ इंद॑वः ॥ ५ ॥

> *dadhiṣvā jaṭhare sutaṃ somamiṃdra vareṇyaṃ |*  
> *tava dyukṣyāsa iṃdavaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दधिष्व । जठरे । सुतं । सोमं । इंद्र । वरेण्यं ।  
> तप । म्यक्षासक । इंदवः ॥ ५ ॥

> *dadhiṣva | jaṭhare | sutaṃ | somaṃ | iṃdra | vareṇyaṃ |*  
> *tapa | myakṣāsaka | iṃdavaḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र वरेण्यं सर्वैर्वर्णीयं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं जठरे दधिष्क
> धारय । इंदवो दीप्ता इमे सोमास्तव द्युक्षासः । दिवि क्षियंति निवसंतीति
> द्युक्षासः । अतस्त्वं तान् धारयेति भावः ॥ दधिष्क । दधातेर्लोटि रूपं । आगमा
> अनुदात्ता इतीटोऽनुदात्तत्वात्पत्ययस्वरः वरेण्यं । वृज् संभक्तौ । वृज एण्य।
> इत्येण्प्रत्ययः । वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तः । द्युक्षासः । क्षियतेरन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यत इति
> डप्रत्ययः । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, hold (दधिष्क (*dadhiṣka*)) in your belly the pressed Soma, which is to be chosen by all. These bright Soma drops are द्युक्षासः (*dyukṣāsaḥ*) (yours): they dwell in heaven (दिवि क्षियंति निवसंतीति (*divi kṣiyaṃti nivasaṃtīti*)). Therefore you should hold them. The meaning is that you should take them in.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*)—esteemed (by all)
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—pressed
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—this Soma juice
- जठरे (*jaṭhare*)—in the stomach
- दधिष्ट्व (*dadhiṣṭva*)—fill
- इंदवः (*iṃdavaḥ*)—these shining Soma juices
- तव (*tava*)—with you
- द्युक्षासः (*dyukṣāsaḥ*)—are such as dwell in the world of the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, fill your stomach with this Soma juice, esteemed by all and pressed by us. These shining Soma juices dwell with you in the world of the gods.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Receive, Indra, into your stomach this excellent effused libation, these bright (drops) abide with you in heaven.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*)—सर्वैर्वणेयं (*sarvairvaṇeyaṃ*). Desired or esteemed by all. Since Soma juice gives joy to those who drink it, everyone desires it. Therefore Soma juice is described here by the word वरेण्यं (*vareṇyaṃ*).

### Printed p. 16

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

द्युक्षासः (*dyukṣāsaḥ*)—दिवि क्षियंति निवसंतीति द्युक्षासः (*divi kṣiyaṃti nivasaṃtīti dyukṣāsaḥ*). Because this Soma juice dwells in the heavenly world, or because the dwelling-place of this Soma juice is heaven, it is described here by the word द्युक्षासः (*dyukṣāsaḥ*), having its dwelling-place in heaven. On this subject there is a sentence of the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa: तृतीयस्यामितो दिवि सोम आसीत् । तं गायत्राहरत् (*tṛtīyasyāmito divi soma āsīt | taṃ gāyatrāharat*). There is a legend that formerly Soma was in heaven, and that Gāyatrī took the form of a hawk and brought it to the earth.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गिर्व॑णः पा॒हि नः॑ सु॒तं मधो॒धारा॑भिरज्यसे ।  
> इंद्र॒ त्वादा॑त॒मिद्यर्श॑ ॥ ६ ॥

> *girvaṇaḥ pāhi naḥ sutaṃ madhodhārābhirajyase |*  
> *iṃdra tvādātamidyarśa ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गिर्वणः । पाहि । नः । सुतं । मधोः । धाराभिः । अज्यसे ।  
> इंद्र । त्वादातं । इत् । यशः ॥ ६ ॥

> *girvaṇaḥ | pāhi | naḥ | sutaṃ | madhoḥ | dhārābhiḥ | ajyase |*  
> *iṃdra | tvādātaṃ | it | yaśaḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> गिर्वणो गीर्भिर्वननीय । तथा च यास्कः । गिर्वणा देवो भवति गीर्भिरेनं
> वनयंति । नि. ६-१४ । इति । तादृशेंद्र नोऽस्मदियं सुतमभिषुतमिमं सोमं
> पाहि । पिब । यतो मधोर्मदकरस्य सोमस्य धाराभिरज्यसे सिच्यसे हे इंद्र
> त्वादातमित्त्वया शोधितं विशदीकृतमेव यशोऽन्नमस्मासु भवति ॥ गिर्वणः । वन
> संभक्तावित्यस्मादसुन् । गिर उपधाया दीर्घाभावश्चांदसः । अमंत्रितस्यॆति षाष्टिकमाद्युदात्तत्वं । पाहि । पा पान इत्यस्माल्लोटि रूपं । आमंत्रितस्याविद्यमानत्वेन
> पादादित्वादनिघातः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । त्वादातं । दैप् शोधने । सत्यपि पकारे नानुबंधकृतमनेजंतत्वमित्येजंत एवायं । तत अदेच इत्यात्वं । अस्मात्कर्मणि क्तः ।
> दाधा घ्नदाबित्यत्रादाबिति प्रतिषेधेन घुसंज्ञाया अभावाद्दो दद्योरिति ददादेतो न भवति । तैति युष्मच्छब्दस्य तृतीया कर्तकरणे कृता बहुलमिति समासः ।
> तृतीया कर्मणीति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः । यशः । अशू व्याप्तौ । अशेयुट्टेत्यसुन् । तत्संनियोगेन धातोर्युडागमः । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*) means one to be served (वननीय (*vananīya*)) with songs of praise. So Yāska (Ni. 6-14) says that a god is गिर्वणा (*girvaṇā*) because people serve him with songs. O Indra, such as you are, drink this pressed Soma of ours, since you are sprinkled with the streams of the intoxicating Soma. O Indra, the food (यशः (*yaśaḥ*)) that is among us is cleansed and made clear by you.

### Printed p. 17

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—O Indra, who are drawn by praises
- नः (*naḥ*)—offered by us
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—and pressed, the Soma
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—drink
- मधोः (*madhoḥ*)—of the intoxicating Soma
- धाराभिः (*dhārābhiḥ*)—with streams
- अज्यसे (*ajyase*)—you are drenched
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- अन्नं (*annaṃ*)—this food in the form of our oblation
- त्वादातमित् (*tvādātamit*)—has been purified by you alone

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, drawn by praises, drink the Soma offered and pressed by us. You are drenched with the streams of the intoxicating Soma. O Indra, this food in the form of our oblation has been purified by you alone.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, you are glorified with praise; drink this our libation, for you are sprinkled with the drops of the exhilarating (draught), the (sacrificial) food is verily purified by you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—गीर्भिर्वननीय (*gīrbhirvananīya*). One who is described by words of praise. Yāska has given the explanation (Ni. 6-14) that a god is called गिर्वणा (*girvaṇā*) because people serve him with songs. That is, the word गिर्वणा (*girvaṇā*) means a god; for by praising him with words of praise and obtaining his favour, he is called गिर्वणा (*girvaṇā*), a god.

मधोः धाराभिः अज्यसे (*madhoḥ dhārābhiḥ ajyase*)—Here the word मधु (*madhu*) means the intoxicating Soma juice, that is, the juice that gives joy. Although the word मधु (*madhu*) by itself means only sweet or joy-giving, since Soma juice has these qualities, using the word मधु (*madhu*) to denote Soma juice is a special custom in the Ṛgveda. धाराभिः (*dhārābhiḥ*) means falling from above or flowing in a stream. When the Soma plants are pressed, the juice pours down from them as water pours down, or, so poured, it flows away and collects in the large wooden vessel, the द्रोण (*droṇa*) or कलश (*kalaśa*), kept for it. The Soma juice that flows and collects in this way is described here by the words मधोः धाराभिः (*madhoḥ dhārābhiḥ*). The seer prays Indra: "Wet your belly with such Soma juice and refresh it."

त्पादातं इत् (*tpādātaṃ it*)—त्वया शोधितं विशदीकृतमेव (*tvayā śodhitaṃ viśadīkṛtameva*). Purified or made clear by you. The commentator says that the word दातं (*dātaṃ*) is formed here from the root दैप् शोधने (*daip śodhane*) and explains the meaning accordingly. But what the food (oblation) purified by Indra is, is not clear. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 18

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

(continued) However, the meaning "given" or "made clear" for दातं (*dātaṃ*) fits to a fair extent. That is, the sense is: this food in the form of an oblation, which we have obtained through Indra's favour.

यशः (*yaśaḥ*)—अन्नं (*annaṃ*). The word यशः (*yaśaḥ*) is read among the 101 names of water (Ni. 2-24), among the twenty-eight names of food (Ni. 3-9) and among the twenty-eight names of wealth (Ni. 3-9). Here the commentator has taken the word यशः (*yaśaḥ*) in the sense of food and explained accordingly. **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि द्यु॒म्नानि॑ व॒निन॒ इंद्रं॑ सचंते॒ अक्षि॑ता ।  
> प्री॒त्वी सोम॑स्य वावृधे ॥ ७ ॥

> *abhi dyumnāni vanina iṃdraṃ sacaṃte akṣitā |*  
> *prītvī somasya vāvṛdhe ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । द्युम्नानि । वनिनः । इंद्रं । सचंते । अक्षिता ।  
> पीत्वी । सोमस्य । ववृधे ॥ ७ ॥

> *abhi | dyumnāni | vaninaḥ | iṃdraṃ | sacaṃte | akṣitā |*  
> *pītvī | somasya | vavṛdhe ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वनिनः । वनं देवताविषयं संभजनमस्यास्तीति वनी । तस्य देवान् संभजमानस्य यजमानस्य द्युम्नानि द्योतमानान्यक्षिता क्षयरहितानि संपूर्णानि सोमादीनि हवींषींद्रमभि अभितः सचंते । समवयंति । तेषु मध्यॆ सोमस्य पीत्वी
> सोमं पीत्वा वावृधे । स इंद्रो वर्धते ॥ वनिनः । अत इनिठनानितीनिप्रत्ययः ।
> तस्य स्वरः । सचंते । षच समवाय इत्यस्य लट रूपं । अक्षिता । क्षि क्षय इत्यस्य निष्ठा ।
> अत्र ण्यदर्थ इति दीर्घाभावः । तस्मादेव क्षियो दीर्घादिति निष्ठानत्ताभावः । नञा
> समासे तस्य स्वरः । पीत्वी । पा पान इत्यस्मात् क्वाप्रत्ययस्य स्नात्वादयश्चेति
> निपातनात् त्वी इत्यादेशः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । घुमास्थागापेत्यादिना ईत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*): one who has वनं (*vanaṃ*), devotion directed to the deities, is वनी (*vanī*); of that sacrificer who serves the gods, the shining, undiminished (अक्षिता (*akṣitā*)), full oblations, Soma and the others, gather around (अभि (*abhi*)) Indra, they join with him (सचंते (*sacaṃte*), समवयंति (*samavayaṃti*)). Among them, having drunk the Soma (पीत्वी (*pītvī*) for सोमं पीत्वा (*somaṃ pītvā*)), that Indra grows (वावृधे (*vāvṛdhe*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*)—of the sacrificer who worships the deity
- द्युम्मानि (*dyummāni*)—shining
- अक्षिता (*akṣitā*)—undiminished and full, the Soma and other oblations
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- अभि (*abhi*)—all around
- सचंते (*sacaṃte*)—pervade and join
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीर्वी (*pīrvī*)—by drinking
- वावृधे (*vāvṛdhe*)—Indra grows

### Printed p. 19

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Soma and other oblations offered by the sacrificer who worships the deity, shining and undiminished and full, pervade and gather around Indra. By drinking the Soma juice Indra grows.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The brilliant and imperishable (Soma juice), offered by the worshipper, encompasses Indra ; having drunk of the libation, he increases (in vigour).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The meaning of this Ṛk does not fit without supplying words. Therefore the commentator has taken the words वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*) and अक्षिता (*akṣitā*) in their component senses. The word वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*), formed from the root वन संभक्तौ (*vana saṃbhaktau*), he explains as "of the sacrificer who attains or worships (the deity)", and the word अक्षिता (*akṣitā*) as indicating "imperishable or complete oblation-materials such as Soma". Although such an interpretation seems necessary to make this Ṛk clear, one should note that the Ṛk can be explained in other ways. But it should be understood that only Indra's drinking of Soma is clearly stated in this Ṛk.

पीत्वी (*pītvī*)—पीत्वा (*pītvā*). Having drunk; here the word पीत्वी (*pītvī*) is used instead of पीत्वा (*pītvā*). Likewise in the Ṛk
उभे सहस्वती भूत्वी सपत्नीं मे सहावहै । (*ubhe sahasvatī bhūtvī sapatnīṃ me sahāvahai |*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-145-5)
the word भूत्वी (*bhūtvī*) is used instead of भूत्वा (*bhūtvā*). But such usages are not frequent in the Ṛgveda.

सोमस्य (*somasya*)—सोमं (*somaṃ*). Here the genitive case is used in place of the accusative. Therefore the word सोमस्य (*somasya*) must be given the accusative sense, "Soma, the Soma juice". Or सोमस्य (*somasya*) may be explained as "a small portion of the prepared Soma juice". This meaning is the more suitable. Such usages are a peculiarity of the Ṛgveda literature.

### Printed p. 20
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒र्वा॒वतो॑ न॒ आ ग॑हि परा॒वत॑श्च वृत्रहन् ।  
> इ॒मा जु॑षस्व नो॒ गिरः॑ ॥ ८ ॥

> *arvāvato na ā gahi parāvataśca vṛtrahan |*  
> *imā juṣasva no giraḥ ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्वावतः । नः । आ । गहि । परावतः । च । वृत्रहन् ।  
> इमाः। जुषस्व । नः । गिरः ॥ ८ ॥

> *arvāvataḥ | naḥ | ā | gahi | parāvataḥ | ca | vṛtrahan |*  
> *imāḥ| juṣasva | naḥ | giraḥ ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृत्रहन्निंद्र अर्वावतोऽर्वाचीनात्समीपाद्देशात्परावतोऽतिदूराद्देशाद्वा नोऽस्मानभिलक्ष्या गहि । आगच्छ । आगत्य च नोऽस्मदीया इमाः स्तुतिलक्षणा
> गिरो वाचो जुषस्व । सेवस्व ॥ परावतः । उपसर्गाच्छंदसि धात्वर्थ इति वतिः ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says: O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, come towards us, whether from a near place (अर्वावतः (*arvāvataḥ*), "from this side") or from a very distant place (परावतः (*parāvataḥ*)). Having come, accept (जुषस्व (*juṣasva*), "serve") these praise-words (गिरः (*giraḥ*), "speech") of ours.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृत्रहन् (*vṛtrahan*)—O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra
- अर्वावतः (*arvāvataḥ*)—whether from a near place
- परावतः (*parāvataḥ*)—or from a distant place
- नः (*naḥ*)—towards us
- आ गहि (*ā gahi*)—be gracious to come
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- इमाः गिरः (*imāḥ giraḥ*)—these words in the form of praise
- जुषस्व (*juṣasva*)—accept

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, whether from near or from a distant place, be gracious to come towards us. Accept these words of ours, which take the form of praise.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Slayer of Vritra, whether from nighl or from afar, come to us, accept these our praises.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Arvāvataḥ, Parāvataḥ—The word arvāk or arvāke is read among the names of nearness (Ni. 3-9), and the word parāvataḥ among the names of distance (Ni. 3-20). The meaning of this Ṛk is easy. Indra, the destroyer of Vṛtra, whether he is near or far, that is, wherever he may be, is prayed to come towards us (to our vicinity) and hear the praises we offer.

### Printed p. 21
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदं॑त॒रा प॑रा॒वत॑नुर्वा॒वतं॑ च हू॒यसे॑ ।  
> इंद्रे॒ह तत॒ आ ग॑हि ॥ ९ ॥

> *yadaṃtarā parāvatanurvāvataṃ ca hūyase |*  
> *iṃdreha tata ā gahi ‖ 9 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । अंतरा । परावतं । अर्वावतं । च । हूयसे ।  
> इंद्र । इह । ततः । आ । गहि ॥ ९ ॥

> *yat | aṃtarā | parāvataṃ | arvāvataṃ | ca | hūyase |*  
> *iṃdra | iha | tataḥ | ā | gahi ‖ 9 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र यद्यस्मिन्नरावतमतिविप्रकृष्टस्य देशस्यार्वावतं संनिकृष्टस्य देशस्य
> चांतरा तयोर्मध्यदेशे हूयसे सम्यगिज्यसे ततस्तस्माद्देशादिहास्मिन्यज्ञ आ गहि ।
> सोमपानार्थवागच्छ ॥ परावतं अर्नावतं । अंतरांतरेण युक्ते । पा. २-३-४ ।
> इति द्वितीया । ततः । पंचम्यास्तसिल् । लित्त्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, when you are invoked (हूयसे (*hūyase*), "properly worshipped") from the very distant region (परावतं (*parāvataṃ*)), from the near region (अर्वावतं (*arvāvataṃ*)), or from the middle region between the two (अंतरा (*aṃtarā*)), then from that region come here, to this sacrifice, to drink the Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—from whichever
- परावतं (*parāvataṃ*)—from a very distant region
- अर्वावतं (*arvāvataṃ*)—or from a very near region
- च (*ca*)—and also
- अंतरा (*aṃtarā*)—from the middle region between these
- हूयसे (*hūyase*)—when you are called
- ततः (*tataḥ*)—from that region
- इह (*iha*)—to this sacrifice
- आ गहि (*ā gahi*)—be gracious to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, whether you are called from a very distant region, from a very near region, or from the middle region between these, from that region be gracious to come to this sacrifice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Whether you are called from nigh or from afar, or from mid space, come hither, Indra, from thence.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

What was said in the previous Ṛk is said again here. Wherever Indra may be, even in the region lying between the far and the near, he is prayed to come to us when we call.

### Printed p. 22
## Sūkta 41 (Maṇḍala 3)
Anuvāda: This Sūkta beginning आ तू न इंद्र (*ā tū na iṃdra*) is the third Sūkta in the fourth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. Viśvāmitra is the seer of this Sūkta, Indra the deity, Gāyatrī the metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is listed as beginning आ तू न (*ā tū na*). In the second Paryāya of the sacrifice called Atirātra, this Sūkta and the next are applied (viniyoga) to the Śastra mantras to be recited by the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin; this is set out by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the sūtra आ तू न इंद्र मद्रगिति सूक्ते (*ā tū na iṃdra madragiti sūkte*) (Ā. Sū. 6-4).

Sūkta 41; Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 4; Sūkta 41. Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 3; Vargas 3, 4. Number of Ṛks in the Sūkta: 9. Seer: Viśvāmitra. Deity: Indra. Metre: Gāyatrī.
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ तू न॑ इंद्र म॒द्र॑ग्घुवा॒नः सोम॑पीतये ।  
> हरि॑भ्यां याह्यद्रिवः ॥ १ ॥

> *ā tū na iṃdra madragghuvānaḥ somapītaye |*  
> *haribhyāṃ yāhyadrivaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अ । तु । नः । इंद्र । मद्रक् । हुवानः । सोमपीतये ।  
> हरिभ्यां । याहि । अद्रिवः ॥ १ ॥

> *a | tu | naḥ | iṃdra | madrak | huvānaḥ | somapītaye |*  
> *haribhyāṃ | yāhi | adrivaḥ ‖ 1 ‖*

### Printed p. 23

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अद्रिवो हे वज्रन् हे इंद्र हुवानो होतृभिराहूयमानस्त्वं
> मद्रक् मदभिमुक्ति सन् नोऽस्मदीये यज्ञे सोमपीतये सोमपानार्थं
> हरिभ्यामशिभ्यां सह तु क्षिप्रिमा याहि ॥ तु । ऋचि तनुघत्यादिना संहितायां
> दीर्घः । मद्रक् । मामंचतीति ऋत्विगधृगित्यादिना क्विन् । प्रत्ययोत्तरपदयोश्चेत्यस्मच्छब्दस्यैकवचने मपर्यंतस्य मादेशः । विश्वगेवयोश्च टेरदृंचतौ
> वप्रत्यय इति टेरदि इत्यादेशः । अद्रिसध्योरंतोदात्तत्वनिपातनं कृत्स्वरनिवृत्यर्थमिति वचनादद्रा देशोऽंतोदात्तः । यणादेशे कृत उदात्तस्वरितयोर्यण इति
> यणः स्वरितत्वं । क्विन्प्रत्ययस्य कुरिति कुत्वं । सोमपीतये ! दासीभारादित्वात्पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । आद्रिवः । मतुवसोरिति रुत्वं । आमंत्रितत्वान्निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Adrivat, O wielder of the thunderbolt, O Indra: being called by the Hotṛs, and turning towards me (मद्रक् (*madrak*), "facing me"), come quickly, together with your two horses (हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)), to our sacrifice to drink the Soma. [Sāyaṇa then gives the grammar of तु (*tu*), मद्रक् (*madrak*), अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*), सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*) and the accent; omitted here.]

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रिवः (*adrivaḥ*)—holder of the thunderbolt weapon
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- हुवानः (*huvānaḥ*)—you who are called (by the Hotṛs)
- मद्रक् (*madrak*)—turned towards me
- नः (*naḥ*)—in this sacrifice performed by us
- सोमपीतये (*somapītaye*)—in order to drink the Soma juice
- हरिभ्यां (*haribhyāṃ*)—with your two horses
- तु (*tu*)—quickly
- आ याहि (*ā yāhi*)—be gracious to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bearer of the thunderbolt weapon, you who are prayed to and called by the Hotṛs, turned towards me, come quickly with your two horses to this sacrifice performed by us, in order to drink the Soma juice.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Indra, wielder of the thunderbolt, come to me with your horses, when invoked to drink the Soma at our (sacrifice).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Adrivaḥ—the meaning is वज्रिन् (*vajrin*), that is, O Indra, who bear the thunderbolt weapon. Yāska, explaining the meaning of the mantra
यदिंद्र चित्र मेहनास्ति त्वादातमद्रिवः । राधस्तन्नो विदद्वस उभयां स्त्या भर ॥ (*yadiṃdra citra mehanāsti tvādātamadrivaḥ | rādhastanno vidadvasa ubhayāṃ styā bhara ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-39-1), says of the word adrivaḥ:
अद्रिवान् । अद्रिरादृणात्येतेन । अपि वात्ते: स्यात् । ते सोमाद इति ह विज्ञायते ॥ (*adrivān | adrirādṛṇātyetena | api vātte: syāt | te somāda iti ha vijñāyate ‖*) (Ni. 4-4)

### Printed p. 24
He thus gives the etymology. That is: because it splits everything, being very hard like a mountain rock, adri means the thunderbolt weapon; or else the word adri may be derived from the root अन भक्षणे (*ana bhakṣaṇe*) ("to eat"). In this context, as a hard stone "eats" other things, that is, destroys them or crushes them to powder, so the thunderbolt is a weapon of hard nature like such a stone. On this point the example is ते सोमाद इति ह विज्ञायते (*te somāda iti ha vijñāyate*); the idea is that as the stones used for crushing Soma plants grind the Soma plants and make the juice flow out, so the thunderbolt, of hard nature, destroys enemies. One who holds such a thunderbolt in his hand is called adrivān. Since the thunderbolt is Indra's weapon, adrivān means Indra. The word adrivaḥ is the vocative singular. The word adri is also read among the names of the cloud (Ni. 3-21).

Haribhyām—Indra's horses are called Harī. Indra has two horses. Since haribhyām is said here, Indra is prayed to come seated in the chariot to which the two horses are yoked. Yāska, describing the vehicles of various deities, says:
हरी इंद्रस्य । रोहितोऽग्ने । हरित आदित्यस्य । रासभावश्विनेः । अजा पूष्णः । पृषत्यो मरुतां । अरुण्यो गाव उषसः । श्यावा सवितुः । विश्वरूपा बृहस्पतेः । नियुतो नायोः । (*harī iṃdrasya | rohito 'gne | harita ādityasya | rāsabhāvaśvineḥ | ajā pūṣṇaḥ | pṛṣatyo marutāṃ | aruṇyo gāva uṣasaḥ | śyāvā savituḥ | viśvarūpā bṛhaspateḥ | niyuto nāyoḥ |*)
(Ni. 3 2-28)**NOTE**
and thus states that Indra's vehicle is the two horses called Harī.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒त्तो होता॑ न ऋ॒त्विय॑स्तिस्थि॒रे ब॒र्हिरा॑नु॒षक् ।  
> अयु॑ज्रन्प्रा॒तरद्र॑यः ॥ २ ॥

> *satto hotā na ṛtviyastisthire barhirānuṣak |*  
> *ayujranprātaradrayaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सत्तः । होता । नः । यत्त्वियः । तिस्तिरे । बर्हिः । आनुषक् ।  
> अयुज्रन् । प्रातः । अद्रयः ॥ २ ॥

> *sattaḥ | hotā | naḥ | yattviyaḥ | tistire | barhiḥ | ānuṣak |*  
> *ayujran | prātaḥ | adrayaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 25

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र नोऽस्मदीये यज्ञॆ होता ऋत्विक् तवाह्वानार्थमृत्वियः प्राप्तकालः सन् सत्तो निषण्णो वर्तते । तथा बर्हिरानुषक् अनुसक्तं परस्परसंबद्धं
> यथा भवति तथा तिस्तिरे । हविष्वासन्नेषु स्तीर्णमभूत् । तथा प्रातः प्रातःसवने
> सोमाभिषवार्थमद्रयो ग्रावाणोऽयुज्रन् । परस्परं संगता अभूवन् । तस्मात्सोमपानार्थं त्वमागच्छेति भावः ॥ सत्तः । षद्ल विशरणादिष्ठित्यस्माद्यर्थाकर्मकश्लिषेत्यादिना । पा. ३-४-७२ । कर्तरि क्तः । सर्वविधीनां छंदसि विकल्पितत्वादत्र
> निष्ठातकारस्य नत्वाभावः । ऋत्वियः । ऋतुशब्दाच्छंदसि घसिति घस्पृत्ययः । घस्येयादेशः । सितीति पदसंज्ञायामोर्गुण इति भसंज्ञाधीनो गुणो न भवति ।
> यणादेशः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । तिस्तिरे । स्त्रञ् अच्छादन इत्यस्य कर्मणि लट्युत
> इद्दा तोरितीत्वं । द्विर्वचने शर्पूर्वाः खय इति तकारस्य शेषः । लिटस्तझयोरेशिरेजिति तप्रत्ययस्यॆशित्यादेशः । पादारित्पादनिघातः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । आनुषक् । षन्ज
> संगे । क्विप् । अनिदितामिति नलोपः । आजन्वोरुपसर्गयोः प्राक्षयोगः । गतिसमासः । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः । आयुज्रन् । युजिर् योग इत्यस्य लुजः ब्बीरिरितो
> वेत्यजादेशः । बहुलं छंदसीति रुडागमः । पादादित्वादनिघातः । अडागमस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, in our sacrifice the Hotṛ priest, having reached the proper time (ऋत्वियः (*ṛtviyaḥ*)) for your invocation, sits ready (सत्तः (*sattaḥ*)). The sacred grass (बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)) has been spread (तिस्तिरे (*tistire*)) joined together (आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*)), one piece close to the next, on the seats of the oblations. And at the morning pressing the stones (अद्रयः (*adrayaḥ*)) for pressing the Soma have been brought together (अयुज्रन् (*ayujran*)), meeting one another. Therefore come to drink the Soma; such is the sense. [The rest of the block is grammar of सत्तः (*sattaḥ*), ऋत्वियः (*ṛtviyaḥ*), तिस्तिरे (*tistire*), आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*), आयुज्रन् (*āyujran*) and accents; omitted here.]

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) नः (*naḥ*)—in this sacrifice of ours
- होता (*hotā*)—the Hotṛ
- ऋत्वियः (*ṛtviyaḥ*)—at the right time of your invocation (at the right time of calling you to the sacrifice)
- सत्तः (*sattaḥ*)—is seated
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—the darbha grass
- आनुषक् (*ānuṣak*)—as if joined to one another
- तिस्तिरे (*tistire*)—has been spread on the altar (in the respective places)
- अद्रयः (*adrayaḥ*)—the stones for pressing the Soma
- आयुज्रन् (*āyujran*)—have been placed together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, in our sacrifice the Hotṛ has come and sat down at the right time, when he prays to you and calls you. The seats of darbha grass have been spread all around the altar. The stones for pressing the Soma juice have been placed together.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
The invoking priest is seated at the proper season; the sacred grass woven together has been spread; the stones are placed in contact for the morning (libation).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Naḥ hotā ṛtviyaḥ sattaḥ—here naḥ may be given the meaning "our", or else, supplying the word यज्ञे (*yajñe*), the meaning

### Printed p. 26
"in our sacrifice" may be stated. For the word ṛtviyaḥ the commentator explains प्राप्तकालः सन् (*prāptakālaḥ san*), "having come at the right time". That is: the sacrifice begins in the morning; the Prātaḥsavana as well, that is, the rite of pressing the Soma juice, takes place in the morning itself. Therefore the priest called Hotṛ, who invokes the gods in the sacrifice, has come and sits on the sacrificial ground at the proper time, not late, that is, in the morning itself, in order to do his duty; such is the idea.

Tistire barhiḥ ānuṣak—[Sāyaṇa:] परस्परसंबद्धं यथा भवति तथा बर्हिःष्वासन्नेषु स्त्रीण मभूत् (*parasparasaṃbaddhaṃ yathā bhavati tathā barhiḥṣvāsanneṣu strīṇa mabhūt*) ("spread on the seats of the oblations in such a way that the blades are joined to one another"). In this sacrifice the darbha grass called barhiḥ, cut evenly, has been spread joined together, so that the gods may come to the sacrificial ground and sit; that is, the darbhas are not spread far apart. They are joined to one another, laid layer upon layer, one layer over another, spread so that the seat is soft. Three layers are thus spread one over the other and the seat is prepared to be soft. This is called barhiḥ. For the word ānuṣak Yāska says: आनुषगिति नामानुपूर्वस्यानुषक्तं भवति (*ānuṣagiti nāmānupūrvasyānuṣaktaṃ bhavati*); and the Nigama also is स्त्रणंति बर्हिरानुषक् (*straṇaṃti barhirānuṣak*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-45-1), (Ni. 6-14). Ānuṣak means mutually, or without a gap, joined one to another; the idea is "not sparse, pressed close". To show that this word is used when darbha is spread pressed close together, Yāska cites the Ṛk स्तृणंति बर्हिरानुषक् (*stṛṇaṃti barhirānuṣak*).

Adrayaḥ ayujran—ग्रावाणः परस्परं संगता अभूवन् (*grāvāṇaḥ parasparaṃ saṃgatā abhūvan*) ("the stones have come together with one another"): the stones for pressing the Soma juice have been placed one beside another; such is the idea.

Now everything is ready in the sacrifice we perform. The priest called Hotṛ has come at the right time to invoke the gods, has sat in his place and is ready. The darbhas have been spread close together, and a soft seat is ready for the gods to come to the sacrificial ground and sit. The stones for crushing the Soma plants and pressing the juice have been placed close by. Since everything is thus ready, O Indra, he is prayed to come quickly to our sacrificial ground to drink the Soma.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा ब्रह्म॑ ब्रह्मवाहः क्रि॒यंत॒ आ ब॒र्हिः सी॑द ।  
> वी॒हि शू॑र पुरो॒ळाशं॑ ॥ ३ ॥

> *imā brahma brahmavāhaḥ kriyaṃta ā barhiḥ sīda |*  
> *vīhi śūra puroḷāśaṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

### Printed p. 27
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमा । ब्रह्म । ब्रह्मवाहः । क्रियंते । आ । बर्हिः । सीद ।  
> वीहि । शूर । पुरोळाशं ॥ ३ ॥

> *imā | brahma | brahmavāhaḥ | kriyaṃte | ā | barhiḥ | sīda |*  
> *vīhi | śūra | puroḷāśaṃ ‖ 3 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बह्माणि स्तोत्राणि स्तुत्यतया वहतीति ब्रह्मवाहा ब्रह्मणा स्तोत्रेण प्राप्यत
> इति ना । हे ब्रह्मवाह इंद्र तुभ्यमिमेमानि ब्रह्म ब्रह्माणि स्तोत्राण्यस्माभिः
> क्रियंते । अतो बर्हिरा सीद । स्तुत्यतया बर्हिषि यज्ञ उपनिश । हे शूर समर्थेंद्रास्माभिर्दीयमानमिमं पुरोडाशं पुरोडाशाख्यं हनिर्विहि । भक्षय ॥
> ब्रह्मवाहः । वह प्रापण इत्यस्माद्वहिहाधाञ्छंदसीत्यसुन् । णिदित्यनुवृत्तेरुपधावृद्धिः । आमंत्रितत्वान्निघातः । क्रियंते । करोतेः कर्मणि यक् । रिज् शयग्लिंक्षिति रिणादेशः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । पादादित्पादनिघातः । बर्हिः । कालाध्वनोरत्यंतसंयोगे द्वितीया । वीहि । वी कांत्यादिषु । लोटि रूपं । हेर्जित्वादुदात्तः
> पुरोळाशं । दाश्य दान इत्यस्मात्पुरस्पूर्वान्मंत्रे श्वेतवहोक्तशस्पुरोडाशो
> ण्वन् । पुरो दाशंत एनमिति । निपातनाद्दकारस्य ळकारः । कृदुत्तरपदस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ब्रह्मवाहः (*brahmavāhaḥ*) is explained as one who carries *brahmāṇi*, hymns of praise, as objects of praise; or as one who is reached by *brahma*, a hymn. "O Indra, bearer of prayers, these *brahma*, that is, hymns, are made by us for you. Therefore sit down on the sacred grass; take your seat on the *barhis* at the sacrifice, being praised. O hero, O capable Indra, eat this oblation called *purodāśa*, which is being given by us."

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ब्रह्मवाहः (*brahmavāhaḥ*)—O Indra, who, pleased by praises, approach us
- इमा ब्रह्म (*imā brahma*)—these praises
- क्रियंते (*kriyaṃte*)—are made by us (therefore)
- बर्हिः (*barhiḥ*)—upon the seat of darbha grass
- आ सीद (*ā sīda*)—sit down
- शूर (*śūra*)—O capable Indra
- पुरोडाशं (*puroḍāśaṃ*)—this oblation in the form of *purodāśa*
- वीहि (*vīhi*)—eat

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who, pleased by praises, approach us: these praises have been made by us. Therefore sit down on the seat of darbha grass. O capable Indra, eat this oblation in the form of *purodāśa*.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Bearer of prayers, these prayers are offered (by us): sit down on the sacred grass : partake, hero, of the oblation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ब्रह्मवाहः (*brahmavāhaḥ*)—This is the vocative singular. It is used to address Indra. *Brahma* means a hymn. Because he comes to our presence, praised with such hymns, he is called by the word ब्रह्मवाहः (*brahmavāhaḥ*).

### Printed p. 28
इमा ब्रह्म क्रियंते (*imā brahma kriyaṃte*)—इमानि स्तोत्राणि अस्माभिः क्रियंते । (*imāni stotrāṇi asmābhiḥ kriyaṃte |*) These hymns are made by us. Although the singular *brahma* is used here, it must be taken to mean the plural *brahmāṇi*. For the word इमाः (*imāḥ*) that qualifies it and the verb क्रियंते (*kriyaṃte*) are both plural, so the word *brahma* too must be regarded as plural.
आ बर्हिः सीद (*ā barhiḥ sīda*)—Sit down on this seat of darbha grass that has been prepared.
शूर पुरोळाशं वीहि (*śūra puroḷāśaṃ vīhi*)—O capable Indra, eat this oblation called *purodāśa* that we now offer you. *Purodāśa* means पुरो दाशंत एनमिति । निपातनाद्दकारस्य ळकारः (*puro dāśaṃta enamiti | nipātanāddakārasya ḷakāraḥ*): because this is offered to the gods in front of the sacrificial altar, this oblation is called *purodāśa*.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> रा॒रं॒भि सव॑नेषु ण ए॒षु स्तोमे॑षु वृत्रहन् ।  
> उ॒क्थेष्टिं॑द्र गिर्वणः ॥ ४ ॥

> *rāraṃbhi savaneṣu ṇa eṣu stomeṣu vṛtrahan |*  
> *uktheṣṭiṃdra girvaṇaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ररंधि । सवनेषु । नः । एषु । स्तोमेषु । वृत्रहन् ।  
> उक्षेषु । इंद्र । गिर्वणः ॥ ४ ॥

> *raraṃdhi | savaneṣu | naḥ | eṣu | stomeṣu | vṛtrahan |*  
> *ukṣeṣu | iṃdra | girvaṇaḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे गिर्वणो गीर्भिः स्तुतिभिर्वननीय वृत्रहन् वृत्रस्य हंतर्हे इंद्र
> नोऽस्माकं संबंधिषु त्रिषु सवनेष्वेषु क्रियमाणेषु सोमेषु सोत्रेषूक्षेषु शस्त्रेषु च रारंधि । रमस्व ॥ रारंधि । रमतेर्लोटि बहुलं छंदसीति शपः
> श्लुः । छंदस्युभयथेति हेरार्धधातुकत्वेनाजित्तादजतश्चति हेर्धिः । सवनेषु ।
> सूयते सोमो येष्वत्यधिकरणे सुनोतेल्र्युट् । लित्स्वरः । णः । नसो नकारस्य
> नश्च धातुस्थोरुषभ्य इति संहितायां णत्वं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, to be won (*vananīya*) by songs, that is, by praises; O slayer of Vṛtra, take your pleasure in these three pressings of ours, and in the Soma-hymns (*stoma*) and the recitations (*ukthā*, *śastra*) that are performed in them." रारंधि (*rāraṃdhi*) means "take delight". सवनेषु (*savaneṣu*) means those in which Soma is pressed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—you who are pleased by praises
- वृत्रहन् (*vṛtrahan*)—and are the slayer of Vṛtra
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—performed by us
- सवनेषु (*savaneṣu*)—in the three pressings too
- विषु (*viṣu*)—(broken in print; continues on the next page) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 29
- पल्पडुव (*palpaḍuva*)—(…performed) **NOTE**
- स्तोमेषु (*stomeṣu*)—by praises in the form of *stoma*
- उक्षेषु (*ukṣeṣu*)—by the *stoma* in the form of *śastra*
- रारंभि (*rāraṃbhi*)—take delight (be satisfied)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who are pleased by praises and are the slayer of Vṛtra: in the three pressings performed by us, take delight, and obtain satisfaction, through the praises in the form of *stoma* that are made here and through the praises in the form of *śastra*.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Slayer of Vritra, Indra, you are gratified by praise, be pleased with these our praises and prayers at our (daily) secrifices.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—गीर्भिः स्तूतिभिर्वननीय । (*gīrbhiḥ stūtibhirvananīya |*) One who is attained through words in the form of hymns; that is, one who hears the hymns we make, is pleased, and comes into our presence.
वृत्रहन् (*vṛtrahan*)—O Indra, destroyer of Vṛtra. Indra's most prominent heroic deed is the destruction of Vṛtra. Hence calling Indra the destroyer of Vṛtra is common in the Ṛgveda.
सवनेषु (*savaneṣu*)—There are three pressings: the morning pressing, the midday pressing and the third pressing. The Soma plants are crushed with stones, the Soma juice is extracted, filtered and offered to the gods. This work must be done with mantras. In sacrifices it is done in the morning, at midday and in the evening. The day on which this Soma-pressing is done is called the *sutyā* day. We have explained this matter at length before.
स्तोमेषु (*stomeṣu*)—Vedic mantras in the form of hymns. For a detailed explanation of these *stoma*, see what we have explained in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 2, page 138.
उक्षेषु (*ukṣeṣu*)—The Vedic mantras used when invoking the gods are called *śastra* mantras. Ordinarily the word *uktha* is used to denote this kind of mantras and also hymns. For a detailed explanation of this word see Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 8-252, 10-695, 13-421.

### Printed p. 30
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒तयः॑ सोम॒पामु॒रुं रि॒हंति॒ शव॑स॒स्पतिं॑ ।  
> इंद्रं॑ व॒त्सं न मा॒तरः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

> *matayaḥ somapāmuruṃ rihaṃti śavasaspatiṃ |*  
> *iṃdraṃ vatsaṃ na mātaraḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मतयः । सोमसां । उरुं । रिहंति । शवसः । पतिं ।  
> इंद्रं । वत्सं । न । मातरः ॥ ५ ॥

> *matayaḥ | somasāṃ | uruṃ | rihaṃti | śavasaḥ | patiṃ |*  
> *iṃdraṃ | vatsaṃ | na | mātaraḥ ‖ 5 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मतयोऽस्माभिः क्रियमाणाः स्तुतय उरुं महांतं सोमसां
> सोमस्य पातारं शवसो बलस्य पतिमिंद्रं त्वां रिहंति । लिहंति । तत्र दृष्टांतः ।
> वत्सं न । यथा वत्सं मातरो धेनवो लिहंति तद्वत् ॥ मतयः । मन ज्ञान इत्यस्मात्कर्मणि मंत्रे वृषेत्यादिना क्किन्नु दात्तः । उरुं । ऊर्णुञ् आच्छादन इत्यस्मान्महत्यर्थे गम्यमाने महति ह्रस्वश्च । उ. १-३२ । इति कुप्रत्ययो धातोर्ह्रस्वश्च ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरः । रिहंति । लिह आस्वादन इत्यस्य लटि रूपं । रलयोरभेदः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । शवसस्पतिमिति संहितायां षष्ठ्याः पतिपुत्र स्पष्टेत्यादिना विसर्जनीयस्य सत्वं ।
> वत्सं । वद व्यक्तायां वाचि । अस्माद्वृत्यवदिहनीत्यादिना सप्रत्ययः । वदत्यभीक्ष्णं
> मातरमिति वॆत्सो गोबालः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ii

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, the hymns (*mati*) made by us lick you, the great one, the drinker of Soma, the lord of strength (*śavas*)." रिहंति (*rihaṃti*) is लिहंति (*lihaṃti*) (r and l are not distinguished). The illustration is वत्सं न (*vatsaṃ na*): as mother cows lick the calf, so. मतयः (*matayaḥ*) is from the root *man* (to know). उरुं (*uruṃ*) is from *ūrṇuñ* (to cover), with the sense "great" (Uṇ. Sū. 1-32). वत्सं (*vatsaṃ*) is from *vad* (to speak clearly): the calf is one who constantly "speaks" to its mother; a young cow-child.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मतयः (*matayaḥ*)—(O Indra,) the praises made by us
- उरुं (*uruṃ*)—you of mighty form
- सोमपां (*somapāṃ*)—drinker of Soma juice
- शवस्य (*śavasya*)—of strength
- पतिं (*patiṃ*)—lord
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—you, who are Indra
- मातरः (*mātaraḥ*)—cows
- वत्सं न (*vatsaṃ na*)—as they lovingly lick their calves
- रिहंति (*rihaṃti*)—lick (become one with you)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, these praises made by us lick you, who are of mighty form, the drinker of Soma juice, the lord of strength, in the form of Indra, as cows lovingly lick their calves. That is, they become one with you and bring you delight.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Our praises caress the mighty Indra, the drinker of the Soma juice. as cows lick their calves.

### Printed p. 31

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मतयः (*matayaḥ*)—The word मतयः (*matayaḥ*) is formed from the root *man jñāne* and is read among the names of the wise (Ni. 3-9). But here the word *mati* means intellect or mantra. That is, hymn-sentences that we recite with understanding.
शवस्य पतिं (*śavasya patiṃ*)—The word *śavas* is read among the one hundred and one names of water (Ni. 2-24) and among the twenty-eight names of strength (Ni. 3-9). Here it is used in the sense of strength. "Lord of strength" means exceedingly strong.
मातरः वत्सं न रिहंति (*mātaraḥ vatsaṃ na rihaṃti*)—यथा धेनवो वत्सं लिहंति तद्वत् । (*yathā dhenavo vatsaṃ lihaṃti tadvat |*) Just as cows lovingly lick their calves, so the hymns we make lick you (Indra); that is, reach you. The meaning is that the hymns we make with great reverence touch you, Indra, and bring you love and delight. As cows have love for their calves, so we have love (devotion) for Indra; with such love we make our hymns. This is the purport of the illustration.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स मं॑दस्वा॒ व्यंध॑सो॒ राध॑से त॒न्वा॑ म॒हे ।  
> न स्तो॒त्रं॑ नि॒दे क॑रः ॥ ६ ॥

> *sa maṃdasvā vyaṃdhaso rādhase tanvā mahe |*  
> *na stotraṃ nide karaḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सु । मंदस्व । हि ! अंधस । राधसे । तन्वा । महे ।  
> न । स्तोतारं । निदे । करः ॥ ६ ॥

> *su | maṃdasva | hi ! aṃdhasa | rādhase | tanvā | mahe |*  
> *na | stotāraṃ | nide | karaḥ ‖ 6 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र स तथाविधस्त्वं महे महते राधसे धनायांधसोऽस्माभिर्दीयमानस्य सोमस्य पानेन तन्ना शरीरेण सह मंदस्व । हृष्टो भव । हिरत्र समुच्चयार्थः । किं च त्वं स्तोतारं मां निदे निंदाय्कॆ विषयभूतं न करः । मा
> कार्षिः ॥ मंदस्व । मदि स्तुत्यादिष्ठित्यस्य लोटि रूपं । हि चेति निघातप्रतिषेधेन
> भवितव्यमिति चेन्न । तत्राप्रातिलोम्य इति वर्तते । अत्र हेः समुच्चयार्थत्वान्नि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you, such as you are, rejoice together with your body (तन्ना (*tannā*)) on drinking the Soma (*andhas*) given by us, for great wealth (*rādhas*). Be glad. हि (*hi*) here has the sense of conjunction. And do not make me, your praiser, an object of reproach (निदे (*nide*))." मंदस्व (*maṃdasva*) is the imperative of *mad*, in the sense of praising and the like.

### Printed p. 32

> घातः । तन्ना । उदात्तस्वरितयोरिति स्वरितत्वं । महे । तकारलोपश्छंदसः । बृहन्महतोरुपसंख्यानमिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । निदे । णिदि कुत्सायामित्यस्मात्संपदादिलक्षणो भावे क्विप् । आगमानुशासनस्यानित्यत्वान्नु मभावः । सावेकाच इति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । करः । करोतेर्लुजं ज्लेः क्रमृद्यरुहिभ्यश्चंदसीत्यजादेशः । निघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

निदे (*nide*) is from *ṇid* (to blame). करः (*karaḥ*) is from *kṛ* in the aorist-injunctive sense (the "*luk*" form).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—you, of such greatness
- महे (*mahe*)—abundant
- राधसे (*rādhase*)—for obtaining wealth
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—by drinking Soma (given by us)
- तन्वा (*tanvā*)—with bodily strength
- मंदस्व (*maṃdasva*)—be gladdened
- हि (*hi*)—and
- स्तोतारं (*stotāraṃ*)—me, who praise you
- निदे (*nide*)—as a target of reproach
- न करः (*na karaḥ*)—do not make

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you of great majesty, for the sake of our obtaining wealth, be gladdened, with bodily strength, by drinking the Soma given by us. Do not make me, who praise you, a target of reproach.

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Be exhilarated bodily (by drinking) of the libation which we offer for the sake of ample wealth : expose not your adorer to reproach.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सः (*saḥ*)—Here the word *tad* is used instead of *yuṣmad*. That is, the commentators give the word सः (*saḥ*) the meaning त्वं (*tvaṃ*), "you". For the verb मंदस्व (*maṃdasva*), "be gladdened", in this sentence is in the second person, so even though the subject word सः (*saḥ*) is in the third person, it is proper to give it the sense of the second person.
राधसे (*rādhase*)—The word *rādhas* is read among the twenty-eight names of wealth beginning with *magham*, *rekṣas* (Ni. 3-9), and Yāska has given the etymology "*rādha* is a name for wealth; by it they succeed" (Ni. 4-4).
अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—The word *andhas* is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). As we said before, the word *andhas* is used here to denote Soma juice.
स्तोतारं निदे न करः (*stotāraṃ nide na karaḥ*)—त्वं स्तोतारं मां निंदायै विषयभूतं मा कार्षिः । (*tvaṃ stotāraṃ māṃ niṃdāyai viṣayabhūtaṃ mā kārṣiḥ |*) O Indra, do not let me, who am praising you, become an object of blame. That is, let no one reproach me.

### Printed p. 33
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यमिं॑द्र त्वा॒यवो॑ ह॒विष्मं॑तो जरामहे ।  
> उ॒त त्वम॑स्म॒युवर्॑सो ॥ ७ ॥

> *vayamiṃdra tvāyavo haviṣmaṃto jarāmahe |*  
> *uta tvamasmayuvarso ‖ 7 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । इंद्र । त्वायवः । हविष्मंतः । जरामहे ।  
> उत । त्वं । अस्मयुः । वसो इति । ७ ॥

> *vayaṃ | iṃdra | tvāyavaḥ | haviṣmaṃtaḥ | jarāmahe |*  
> *uta | tvaṃ | asmayuḥ | vaso iti | 7 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वायवो यज्ञार्थं त्वामात्मन इच्छंतस्त्वत्कामाः संतो हनिष्मंतः संभृतहविष्का वयं जरामहे । त्वां स्तुमः । हे वसो सर्वस्य वासक इंद्र
> उत त्वमप्यस्मयुरस्मदीयहविः स्वीकरणार्थमस्मानात्मन इच्छन् भव ॥ त्वायवः ।
> त्वामिच्छंतीत्यर्थे सुपः आत्मनः क्यच् । प्रत्ययोत्तरपदयोश्चति मपर्यंतस्य
> त्वादेशः । क्याच्छंदसीत्युप्रत्ययः । त्वदृव इति प्राप्तौ युष्मदस्मदोरनादेश इत्यविभक्तावपि हलादौ व्यत्ययेनात्वं । प्रत्ययस्वरः । हनिष्मंतः । तसौ मत्वर्थ इति
> भसंज्ञायां सकारस्य रुत्वाभावः । स्वरविधौ व्यंजनमविद्यमानवदिति परिभाषानाश्रयणान्मतुप उदात्तताभावः । इसिप्रत्ययस्वरः । जरामहे । जरतिः स्तुतिकर्मेति
> यास्कः । अस्मयुः : अस्मानिच्छतीत्यर्थे सुपः क्यच् । तस्मादुप्रत्ययः । पूर्वपदांतलोपश्चांदसः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, we desire you for the sake of the sacrifice, longing for you, and with our oblations gathered together we praise you (जरामहे (*jarāmahe*); Sāyaṇa glosses it "we praise you"). O Vasu, you who give dwelling to all, be desirous of us as well, wishing for us so that you may accept our oblation. Sāyaṇa explains त्वायवः (*tvāyavaḥ*) as "those who desire you", and अस्मयुः (*asmayuḥ*) as "one who desires us". He cites Yāska: जरतिः स्तुतिकर्मेति यास्कः (*jaratiḥ stutikarmeti yāskaḥ*) (jarati is a verb of praising).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वायवः (*tvāyavaḥ*)—we who with faith desire your favour
- हविष्मंतः (*haviṣmaṃtaḥ*)—who possess oblations
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- जरामहे (*jarāmahe*)—praise you
- वसो (*vaso*)—O Indra, giver of dwelling to all
- उत त्वं (*uta tvaṃ*)—you also
- अस्मयुः (*asmayuḥ*)—be desirous of accepting our oblation

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, we who with faith desire your favour and who possess oblations praise you. O Indra, giver of dwelling to all, you too be desirous of accepting our oblation.

### Printed p. 34

##### English translation (H. H. Wilson)
Devoted, Indra, to you, and offering oblations, we glorify you, giver of dwellings: be favourably disposed to us.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The meaning of this Ṛk is easy. There are no difficult words and no obscurity of meaning. O Indra, in order to win your favour we have prepared oblations to offer you and are praising you. The main idea is: "You, giver of dwelling to all, come near us and accept the oblation."
#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मारे अ॒स्मद्वि मु॑मुचो॒ हरि॑प्रिया॒र्वाज् या॑हि ।  
> इंद्र स्वधावो मत्तैव ॥ ८ ॥

> *māre asmadvi mumuco haripriyārvāj yāhi |*  
> *iṃdra svadhāvo mattaiva ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । आरे । अस्मत् । वि । मुमुचः । हरिप्रियॆ । अर्वाण् । याहि ।  
> इंद्र । स्वध्वावः । मत्स्य । इह ॥ ८ ॥

> *mā | āre | asmat | vi | mumucaḥ | haripriyè | arvāṇ | yāhi |*  
> *iṃdra | svadhvāvaḥ | matsya | iha ‖ 8 ‖*

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे हरिप्रिय हरी अश्वा प्रियौ यस्य तादृश हे इंद्र अस्मदस्मत्त आरे दूरे
> रथे योजतावश्वा मा वि मुमुचः । मा विमोचय । ताभ्यामुपेत एनार्वाज्
> अस्मदाभिमुख्येन याहि । आगच्छ । हे स्वधावः सोमलक्षणान्नवन्निंद्र इहास्मिन्देवयजने मत्स्य । सोमं पीत्वा हृष्टो भव ॥ आस्मत् । अस्मच्छब्दस्य पंचमीबहुवचनस्य पंचम्या अत् । पा. ७-१-३१ । इत्यदादेशः । मुमुचः । मुच्चै मोक्षण
> इत्यस्य ण्यंतस्य छांदसे लुजि चजि रूपं । अभ्यासस्य दीर्घाभावश्चांदसः । न
> माज्योग इत्यडभावः । निघातः । हरिप्रिय । आमंत्रितस्य पादादित्वात्पाष्ठिकमाद्युदात्तत्वं । मत्स्य । मदि स्तुत्यादिश्वित्यस्य लोटि बहुलं छंदसीति विकरणस्य लुक् ।
> अमंत्रितस्याविद्यमानत्वेन पादादित्वादनिघातः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, "whose two horses (hari) are dear" (हरिप्रिय (*haripriya*)), do not unyoke the two horses yoked to the chariot while you are far from us (मा वि मुमुचः (*mā vi mumucaḥ*): "do not release"). Provided with them, come (याहि (*yāhi*): "arrive") facing us (अर्वाण् (*arvāṇ*)). O Indra, स्वधावः (*svadhāvaḥ*), possessor of food in the form of Soma, rejoice (मत्स्य (*matsya*)) here, in this place of worship of the gods: having drunk Soma, be gladdened.
### Printed p. 35

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हरिप्रिय (*haripriya*)—O Indra, lover of horses
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—from our place of sacrifice
- आरे (*āre*)—far away
- मा वि मुमुचः (*mā vi mumucaḥ*)—do not unyoke the horses from the chariot and let them go
- अर्वाज् (*arvāj*)—facing us (with them)
- याहि (*yāhi*)—graciously come
- स्वधावः (*svadhāvaḥ*)—possessor of food in the form of Soma
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- इह (*iha*)—in this place of sacrifice
- मत्स्य (*matsya*)—be satisfied by drinking Soma

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)
